| ✅ | **The compose card above the conversation, as a choice** | **Built 16 September 2026 on branch `reply-card-choice` and gated green with batch 11 on the two Windows benches.** *Reply card* on the webmail's preferences page, beside the reading pane's: *Under the message it answers* (the default, as before) or *Above the conversation*, kept with the account as `reply_place` so it follows the reader between browsers; an inline reply goes into a slot above the conversation's cards or into the one under the message it answers, and a reply already open moves when the choice is saved. Nothing about the sent message changes - it is top-posted either way. Three texts in all twenty-three catalogues; four checks in the page's harness (the default slot, the choice saved with the account, the next reply written above the conversation and still addressed to the sender, and the choice put back). Asked for in discussion #266 on 15 September 2026, and answered there when it landed. The reply that goes out is already top-posted - the new text above the quoted message - but the compose card sits UNDER the message it answers in the reading pane, with the earlier messages as cards above it, which is Gmail's shape and not Outlook's. One per-reader setting on the webmail's settings page puts the card above the conversation instead, with the current placement as the default; nothing about the sent message changes. |
| ✅ | **Snooze presets and custom swipe actions** (Gmail, Proton) | **Done 15 September 2026.** Settings: what a swipe to the right and to the left does on a touch screen (archive, delete, junk, mark as read, nothing), the hours of the first snooze preset, and the hour of the day for *tomorrow* and *next week* - kept with the account, the snooze menu's captions following. Four harness checks. |
| ✅ | **A mailbox export** (Google Takeout, Proton's export) | **Done 17 September 2026.** The whole mailbox in one download from the person's own Settings page: `GET /api/v1/me/export.zip` is every folder with mail as an mbox, the address book as vCard and the calendars beside it, with the preferences, the filters and the log lines naming the address, and no administrator involved; the per-folder mbox (`GET /api/v1/me/folders/{id}/export`, the Folders page) and the Contacts page's vCard and CSV stay for the smaller ask. The download is held in memory once, so it is refused at 500 MB with a sentence that says an administrator writes the same archive to disk with no ceiling but the format's 4 GB - past what the 200 MB folder download carried, and a limit the row's next step is to lift by streaming. `docs/PersonalData.md`. |
| ✅ | **Import from the old provider** (Proton's Easy Switch, Fastmail's import) | **Done 17 September 2026.** Other accounts in the webmail offers *Sign in to Gmail* and *Sign in to Outlook.com* once an administrator registers the server with the provider: the authorization code flow with PKCE (S256), a state good for ten minutes and once, and a nonce in an HttpOnly SameSite=Lax cookie the unauthenticated `/portal/oauth/callback` checks. The callback exchanges the code, reads the address from the id_token and makes an IMAP fetch account on the provider's server that logs in with XOAUTH2 (`Util/ReaderOAuth.cpp`: the refresh, a token cached for four fifths of its life, a rotated refresh token written back); signing in again gives the existing account the new grant. With the folders brought over, the first collection lists them and brings nothing; the page offers them to tick, the views of the others (`\All`, `\Flagged`, `\Important`: All Mail, Starred, Important in any language) unticked, and the mirror skips what is left out. Schema 6065 (`hm_fetchaccounts.faoauthprovider`, `faoauthtoken` protected, `famirrorskip`, `famirrorseen`); nine `import_oauth_*` settings, the two secrets write-only and protected; an account signed in keeps its server and address. `RestApiImportSignIn` runs the whole flow against a local token endpoint and a scripted IMAP server; ReaderOAuthTester checks base64url and RFC 7636's challenge; 8 harness checks. [Migration.md](hmailserver/docs/Migration.md). |
| ✅ | **Right-to-left languages** | **Done 15 September 2026.** Arabic, Hebrew and Persian - `ar.json`, `he.json`, `fa.json`, the full page (631 keys each when they landed, 647 today, as every catalogue holds) - take the catalogues to twenty-three, and the page is mirrored for them. The direction is decided in the one place every language change passes through, whichever way it arrived (the server's `Accept-Language` block in the page, the choice this browser remembers, the account's preference): `applyCatalogue` writes `lang` and `dir` on the root element. The stylesheet does the rest, because it was rewritten in logical properties - `margin-inline-start`, `padding-inline`, `inset-inline`, `border-inline-end`, the four logical corner radii, `text-align: start` - so one sheet serves both directions and every computed value in English is what it was. A message keeps its own direction: the text pane, a conversation card's body, the subject field, the textarea and the rich editor carry `dir="auto"` and each settle their direction from their own first strong character, which is the only right answer when an Arabic reader opens an English message. The HTML frame carries `dir="auto"` too, but that attribute does not reach the document inside a frame, and the document the server answers for it (`/api/v1/me/messages/{id}/html`) sets no direction of its own - so an HTML message that does not declare `dir` itself is shown left to right whatever its language, and the harness checks only the frame element's attribute; `dir="auto"` on that document's body is still owed. Four things needed an explicit rule and each says why in a comment: the *sign* of an inset `box-shadow`, which has no logical form (the row cursor's bar and a colour rule's bar, through a `--lead` custom property that is 1 or -1); fourteen direction-bearing icons flipped about their view box; the narrow-screen drawer, parked on the other side; and the context menu, which is placed in viewport coordinates and now opens away from the pointer. Deliberately left physical, and commented: the tooltip and the full-screen compose window, both centred and direction-neutral, and the swipe gestures, whose left and right are the reader's fingers. The harness gained ten checks - 274, from 264 - and its own defect was found on the way: its DOM's text nodes had no `nodeType` and no `nodeValue`, so `localiseTree` walked past every one of them and all 264 checks before this had been asserting the English page. The first paint is in the reader's direction too: the server writes the page's `<html>` tag for the catalogue it has just negotiated, so the frame that arrives is already the right way round, and the script writes the same two attributes again when it runs and on every later change. |
| ✅ | **The reader's own time zone** | **Done 15 September 2026.** Settings has a *Time zone* choice - the zones the browser knows, *Where this browser is* as the default - kept with the account; the list's times, the open message's date and the conversation cards render in it, and today and this year are decided in it. Three harness checks. |
| ✅ | **Undo more** | **Done 15 September 2026.** The toast with Undo that filing and sending have, for the three that lacked it: a label taken off a message (Undo puts it back on that message), the rules saved by an addition or a removal (Undo writes the script back as it was), and a contact removed (Undo adds it back). Four harness checks. |

| | Item | Detail |

| ✅ | **The app badge** (every mail app) | **Done 15 September 2026.** `navigator.setAppBadge` with the same total the title carries - the folders the reader asked to be told about - on every count the page reads, cleared at zero; nothing without the API. |
| ✅ | **Share to the webmail** (every mail app) | **Done 15 September 2026.** The manifest declares a share target (`POST /portal/share`, multipart: title, text, url, files - images, PDFs, text, calendars, `.eml`); the service worker answers the POST by putting what was shared into a cache of its own and sends the page to a new message, which reads the cache once, fills the subject and the text, attaches the files as if dropped, and deletes it. |
| ✅ | **The mailto: handler** | **Done 15 September 2026.** Settings offers *Open mailto: links here*, which calls `registerProtocolHandler` for `/portal#/compose?mailto=%s` (the manifest declares the same); the compose route parses the mailto: URL - addresses, subject, body, cc, bcc - into a new message. |
| ✅ | **Offline for the last thirty days** (Gmail offline) | **Done 17 September 2026.** The reader chooses in Settings how many days to keep, under `reader_offline_days_max` (30 by default, 0 offers none, a lowered ceiling clamps), and the page keeps the bodies, the rendered HTML documents and the attachments of the messages received within the window in IndexedDB - as they are opened, and in the background from `GET /api/v1/me/offline/messages`, a listing of ids, folders, sizes, received times and flags that reads no file and copies no message - one pass per page over the folders in memory, holding at most the 501 rows a page may need (after sign-in, after a choice, when the connection returns, on a visit to Settings, and a few seconds after the change probe reports mail arriving or moving) - pruned as the window moves, flags and folders brought into step with the server's; a choice the server could not read is a 500 the page leaves its store alone on, never *nothing chosen*, and the hundred-preference cap is the preferences route's own 400. Read and searched with no connection (subject, sender, recipients, text; `from:`, `to:`, `subject:`, `in:`, `label:`, `category:`, `has:attachment`, `is:`, a phrase, `-word`; `larger:`/`smaller:` against the kept size, `before:`/`after:` and `older_than:`/`newer_than:` against the received time on the browser's clock, `filename:` against the kept attachments' names), the service worker answering a kept message's HTML and attachments under the server's own headers when the network fails, a page opened with no connection showing the kept mail of the last account and asking again when one is back - on the browser's online event, and every thirty seconds otherwise, since a listener that is down fails the same way with the network up and no online event to come - `navigator.storage.estimate` saying when the window will not fit (the newest are kept), the card counting what is kept and clearing it, and all of it forgotten at sign-out. `API/RestApiOffline.cs` (in the Windows suite and linked into the Linux one) and thirty-seven harness checks against an in-memory IndexedDB, the page opened with no connection and the server that could not read the choice among them; [OfflineMail.md](hmailserver/docs/OfflineMail.md). Left out, and said in the doc: the reader's default is nothing (a browser someone else can open must not fill itself unasked; Gmail's is opt-in too); `has:link` and `in_reply_to:` are not answered offline and `OR` is ignored; an attachment above 8 MB, or parts beyond 32 MB for one message, are not kept; a flag set offline is not sent back. The row as written: Offline holds the last listing and an outbox. The last thirty days of bodies and attachments kept in the browser's storage, searched offline, bounded by a setting. |

### Normal users: contacts and the organisation

| | Item | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| ✅ | **The organisation's directory** (Outlook's Global Address List, Google's directory) | **Built 18 September 2026 on branch `row210`, schema 6078; every changed translation unit compiles on its own, the test project builds and every checker passes. `RegressionTests.API.RestApiDirectory` and `RegressionTests.API.DirectoryCom` have not run against a server, and the row is done when they pass.** **Reviewed and repaired the same day:** the completion's `q=` was decoded with the machine's code page and so could never match a name, a title or a department that is not ASCII; the photo route listed the whole domain once per photo and the CardDAV collection built every card with every photo inline on every poll, both of which made a large domain expensive for any signed-in mailbox to ask for; the Control Panel's Directory entry tab threw all nine values away - the opt-out included - if the server refused any one of them, and closed as though it had saved; the webmail's own `Content-Security-Policy` had no `img-src 'self'`, so none of the photos could be drawn at all; PROPPATCH answered 405 where three places said 403 with a reason; clearing somebody else's opt-out left nothing but an application-log line; and the first save of an entry was a read-modify-write that two hands could lose. Every active account and every enabled distribution list of **one** domain - the reader's own, read from the signed-in account and never from the request, so no spelling of any route reads another domain's names - with a job title, a department, three telephone numbers, a photo and the administrator's note, kept in `hm_directory` (one row per account that has one; an account with nothing set has no row and is listed from `hm_accounts` alone, and the row goes with the account and is carried in the backup). `GET /api/v1/me/directory?q=` is the listing, `GET`/`PUT /api/v1/me/directory/me` the person's own entry, `GET /api/v1/me/directory/{id}/photo` and `/me/photo` the photos, and `GET`/`PUT /api/v1/accounts/{address}/directory` the administrator's view with the note - scoped by the address in the path as every other account route is. The webmail has a **Directory** page - a card per person with the photo, the title, the numbers and where they sit, **Write** and **Add to contacts**, a list marked as a list - and its To, Cc and Bcc completion now asks the address book and the directory together, the reader's own contacts first and the directory hits after, each labelled, an address in both offered once as the contact; under Settings, *Your directory entry* is the person's own fields, their photo and the opt-out. CardDAV serves a second, **read-only** `Directory` collection beside the account's own book at `/dav/addressbooks/<address>/directory/`, listed by a PROPFIND on the home, with `getctag`, `sync-token` and the same three reports, `current-user-privilege-set` saying `read` alone, and PUT, DELETE and PROPPATCH refused 403 with the reason - the cards generated from the rows under a UID stable for the address. Nine COM properties on `Account` (`DirectoryTitle` to `DirectoryPhotoType`), a **Directory entry** tab in the Control Panel's account dialog and the same card in the Control Deck's account editor; thirty-two texts in the twenty-three webmail catalogues, eighteen in the Deck's twenty and twenty-four in the Control Panel's seventeen. A photo is a JPEG, a PNG or a GIF of at most 100 KB, **judged by its bytes and never by a media type the sender named**, kept base64 and served from a route of its own so that a listing the completion asks on every keystroke carries none. Two `hm_settings` rows, both **on**: `DirectoryEnabled` (turn it off on a server whose one domain holds people who are not one organisation - with it off the listing is empty and says so, the page and the Settings card are hidden, the completion stops asking and the CardDAV collection stays there with nothing in it, so a subscribed phone syncs to nothing rather than failing every poll) and `DirectoryUserEditable` (off leaves the fields to the administrator). **The opt-out is never what a switch takes away**, and it is not invisibility: out of the listing, the completion, the page and the collection, but mail still arrives and anybody who knows the address can still write - said on every surface that offers it. **Thinner than the row asks, and said in [Directory.md](hmailserver/docs/Directory.md):** it is one domain and there is no cross-domain form, so a company with several domains has several directories; there is no manager, reports-to chain or address-book view as Outlook's GAL has, only a flat list with a department as free text; nothing is learned from Active Directory or LDAP even where the server authenticates against one, and there is no CSV import of entries; there is no LDAP server for a client that wants a directory that way; a distribution list is an address and not even a name, `hm_distributionlists` having no name column at all, with no photo, title or opt-out of its own and its members not listed; and there is no `hmctl` verb of its own, the routes being reachable through `hmctl api`. |
| ✅ | **Collected addresses** (Gmail's "Other contacts", Outlook's suggested recipients) | **Done 17 September 2026.** Every To, Cc and Bcc address of a message the person sends - through the webmail (`POST /api/v1/me/messages`) and, now, from a mail client over an authenticated SMTP submission, under the account that signed in - is added to `hm_contacts` once, marked `source: collected` (`ContactCard::Collect`). A collected address is a suggestion, not a contact: the Contacts page offers it apart under *Other contacts* with *Keep*, the To, Cc and Bcc completion offers both halves, `GET /api/v1/me/contacts?source=manual|collected` lists either, `PUT /api/v1/me/contacts/{id}` with `source: manual` keeps one (the only direction there is), and CardDAV serves only what was kept, so a phone's address book is not filled with everyone ever written to; a phone creating a card for a collected address makes that row the contact under the phone's name rather than being refused. Tested in `API/RestApiSelfService.cs` and `API/CardDav.cs`, drawn by the webmail's harness; `docs/Contacts.md`. |
| ✅ | **Contact photos, birthdays, notes and custom fields** | **Done 17 September 2026.** A card a CardDAV client sends is kept as sent (schema 6040) and served back byte for byte, and the page now reads what it carries: `/api/v1/me/contacts` answers `birthday` (BDAY, as YYYY-MM-DD or --MM-DD), `note`, `phones` (every TEL) and `has_photo` beside the name and address, `GET /api/v1/me/contacts/{id}/photo` serves the embedded photo as the image it is (a base64 PHOTO of vCard 3 or a data: URI of vCard 4), and the Contacts page shows the photo as the avatar, the birthday marked on the day, the phones and the note. `?fields=card` puts the stored card on each contact and the page's vCard export sends it whole, so a card exported from the webmail keeps what the phone stored; a vCard the page imports goes up as `card` on `POST /api/v1/me/contacts` and is kept as sent. A contact is still a name and one address, stored under the card's preferred EMAIL. Tested in `API/CardDav.cs`, drawn by the webmail's harness; `docs/Contacts.md`. |
| ✅ | **Shared address books** (Zimbra, Kerio) | **Done 18 September 2026.** An account keeps several address books (`hm_addressbooks`, schema 6079) and shares one with another account of this server, or with every account of its OWN domain, read-only or writable (`hm_addressbookshares`; the account or the domain, never both, and neither column carries a foreign key, so an account deleted and a domain deleted each take their shares with them by hand). `hm_contacts` gains `contactbookid`, which the upgrade stamps 0 on every existing row and migrates nothing: 0 means *the account's default book*, the book `AddressBookStore::EnsureDefault` makes the first time anything asks that account for its books - and which then stamps its 0 rows with its own id, one statement, once per account - so an installation nobody has looked at behaves exactly as it did and no upgrade script has to read the accounts table. The default book is the account's lowest book id rather than a name, so renaming it is free; it cannot be deleted. A contact of a shared book is the OWNER'S row, read and written under the owner's account id. Shared from the webmail's Contacts page (an Address books card with the books, their contact counts, and a Sharing panel: an address for one account, the sharer's own domain name for all of it, *Can edit* for write; a domain-wide share aimed at another hosted domain is 403, because nobody in a domain can leave a share that was not made to them and the route that removes one is scoped to the owner's domain, so aimed elsewhere it would be an unleavable book in every Contacts page of another domain that neither its people nor its own administrator could take off), listed to the sharee as a second book, searchable by the To, Cc and Bcc completion (`book=all`, the reader's own books first and a shared one labelled with its name), and over CardDAV as extra collections in the addressbook-home - `contacts/` is still the default book, so a client syncing since 6.3 keeps syncing, `book-<id>/` is another of the account's own and `shared-<id>/` is one shared with it, a read-only one refusing PUT and DELETE with 403 and saying so in `current-user-privilege-set`, and a DELETE of the collection giving the share back rather than deleting anybody's contacts, exactly as the calendar does. REST: `/api/v1/me/address-books` CRUD and `/{id}/shares/{target}`, the contacts routes taking `book` (the account's own books when absent, which is what they answered before); an administrator lists an account's books and who each is shared with over `GET /api/v1/accounts/{address}/address-books` and removes one share with a DELETE under it, recorded on the audit trail as `address-book-share-removed`. The books are carried by the backup as a per-account store, with a contact naming its book by NAME rather than by an id a restore reassigns (the new `RoleAddressBook` column role) and an unresolvable name putting the contact in the default book rather than dropping it; the shares are not carried, and `AccountStores.cpp` says why. A failed read is never answered as a fact: an unreadable `hm_addressbookshares` is 500 on both surfaces rather than a partial list that reads like a share taken back or a 404 that makes a phone throw its copy away, a count that could not be made is 500 rather than `"contacts":0`, and the store refuses to delete a book whose default-ness it could not establish. The administrator's read makes nothing - it calls `ListBooksReadOnly` and reports the book an untouched account has not made yet at id 0 - because it needs only `ViewAccounts`, which the auditor role holds, and `check-role-choke-point.py` proves route by route that an auditor changes nothing. `API/RestApiSharedAddressBooks.cs` on ports 9756-9757 and twenty-one checks in the webmail's harness; [SharedAddressBooks.md](hmailserver/docs/SharedAddressBooks.md). **Left out, and said in the doc:** a group is never shared, in either direction and by enforcement rather than by accident - its members resolve against its owner's WHOLE address book, so a group row of a book somebody shares with you is not listed at any `book=`, not found by id and not served over CardDAV, and a group card PUT into a book you do not own is 403 even with write, which is what stops a writable share being a way to ask for the contacts of the books that were not shared or to plant a recipient list the owner's own compose would expand; a card PUT into a shared book for an address the owner collected elsewhere is 409 rather than a silent move of their row into it; one account holds one address once across its books, so a contact is moved between books rather than copied; a contact cannot move between accounts; deleting a book deletes the contacts in it, with no undo; the permission is per book, not per contact, as the calendar's is; the target is one account or one domain of this server - never an account group and never another server; an administrator sees the shape of the sharing and never the contacts, which is on purpose; and neither the Control Panel nor the Control Deck has a page for it. **`API/RestApiSharedAddressBooks` has not run against a server, and this row is done when it passes.** |

### Administrators: day to day

| | Item | Detail |

| ✅ | **Accounts from a spreadsheet** (every hosted admin console) | **Built 15 September 2026 on branch `accounts-csv`, and gated green with batch 10 on 16 September 2026: the file now goes in and comes back out as the same accounts. Every changed translation unit compiles on its own and `build/check-cli.py` passes; `RegressionTests.API.RestApiAccountImport` has not run, and this row is done when it passes.** `hmctl accounts import people.csv --dry-run` makes only GETs and lists what would be made and what would be refused, `hmctl accounts import people.csv` makes them under their domains, `hmctl accounts export example.com accounts.csv` writes them out, and `Import-HmAccount -WhatIf` / `Export-HmAccount` are the same in PowerShell. The columns are the create route's own field names, which are the names the listing answers with: `address` and `password` (required), `first_name`, `last_name`, `active`, `max_size_mb` and `admin_level` - a name is two columns, not one split at a space. `GET /api/v1/domains/<domain>/accounts` answers the six columns an export writes for every account (it answered `address` and `active` only, so every export of a real server had empty columns); `POST /api/v1/domains/<domain>/accounts` reads its body as a typed document and refuses an unknown field or a wrong type by name (it dropped `name` and `admin_level` with a 201, and refused a password sent as a number as missing), holds names to their 60-character columns, puts the password through the password policy as every other door does (it applied none, so an import could make administrators with passwords the Control Panel refuses), and lets a key restricted to named domains make users and domain administrators but refuses it a server administrator with a 403 - asking `HoldsServerAuthority_`, the one definition the update asks too. Both clients send every cell as text except `active` and `max_size_mb`, refuse a column outside the seven - `name` included, with where a name goes - and, before anything is sent and in the create's words, a row with no password, an `active`, `max_size_mb` or `admin_level` it does not take, or a name past 60 UTF-16 code units, so a dry run refuses those rows as the import would; the password policy and the domain's limits are the server's to judge when the row is sent. They trim every cell but the password, and refuse a password with white space at an edge rather than trim it into a different one. They read the file as strict UTF-8, and refuse a row with more cells than its header; an address that already exists is left alone and counted, never overwritten; the export writes no password column. Running the module under Windows PowerShell 5.1 found three faults there, all fixed: a request body sent as ISO-8859-1 and a response read as ISO-8859-1, which broke every name outside ASCII in both directions, and a catch naming a type 5.1 does not have, which replaced every server refusal with "Unable to find type". `build/check-cli.py` answers the listing as the real route does and holds both clients to one spreadsheet - the bodies, their refusals, the server's own refusal, the export and the round trip (437 checks; the same checks fail 29 against the clients as they were before this branch and 11 against the clients before its review added the name width and the password edge, and the import and export checks failed 5 against the old recorded listing). `RestApiAccountImport` holds the server to storing and listing what the import sends, to the refusals and the 403, to the password policy, and to a listed entry making the same account, and runs hmctl and the module against the real server. The Linux suite's Account shim now stores on its first Save the names and the level it used to drop. `hmailserver/docs/CommandLine.md` is the walk. **Not in this row, and not built:** the same import from a page - a file dropped on the Deck or the Control Panel. |
